# Statistics

This file merges the `STATS.tsv` of every unit: one row per statistical test or interval, with the statistical unit, n, whether measurements are distinct or repeated, covariates, correction family, estimate, uncertainty and how it was built, seed, draws and software. The full columns (statistic, df, exact P, effect size and how it was calculated) are in each unit `STATS.tsv`. The reference text is v30: `v30 para` is the paragraph index in `visible_v30f.txt` (column `v30_para` of the unit file for tables and text_statistics; approximate, prefix `~`, for figure units, found by word overlap with the v28d line).

## Design hierarchy

Acquisitions (one LC-MS/MS run) are nested in specimens, specimens in participants (healthy volunteers, CapScan) or patients (PREDICT). A test is done at the level named in the `unit` column. Tests on acquisitions of one specimen, or on specimens of one participant, are never treated as independent; participant or patient blocks are resampled or permuted whole (see the `distinct_or_repeated` and `uncertainty_construction` columns). The v30 roster has 726 acquisitions: 15 CAMPI, 58 healthy stool, 306 PREDICT and 347 CapScan.

Totals: 1037 rows. Statistical units: patient 424, participant 423, acquisition 147, peptide 12, sequence 9, synthetic mixture 9, acquisition pair 7, species 2, sampling design (medians over acquisition pairs) 1, region 1, protein group 1, acquisition-seed run 1.

## Changes in v30 that affect tests

- The original protein grouping was removed; direct peptide assignment is the only grouping rule. The CapScan leading-species agreement (Fig. 5f) changes from 45.6% to 46.4% (all eligible) and from 60.0% to 57.6% (strict); recount: `pipelines/peptide_centric_downstream/strict_subset_direct_recount.py`.
- The EDS held-out prediction family is the four direct-assignment model tests (was eight grouping-by-model tests). Benjamini-Hochberg q over four tests: protein 0.0067, baseline 0.050, combined 0.0067, distance 0.0067 (unchanged at the printed precision); `pipelines/predict_eds_heldout_prediction/eds_bh_direct4.py`.
- Second search engine (MSFragger 4.1 and Percolator 3.06.5) extended to 291 of 296 PREDICT, 281 of 282 CapScan and 58 healthy-stool acquisitions; `pipelines/clinical_second_engine`.

- TEXT-GAP: exact P and q of the Fig. 6a,b tests are not printed: P = 6.1035e-05 (2 of 32,768 sign vectors, exact), q = 0.000290 for all five legend tests (STATS.tsv rows 1-5); the figure prints "q < 0.001".

- TEXT-GAP: seeds are not stated. A24: test exact (no seed); bootstrap weights from numpy default_rng seeded with the first 4 bytes (little-endian) of sha256("bootstrap|n|draws|20260914"), drawn once per n. A28: rng(20260914 + n) for random signs (n > 16), rng(20261014 + n) for the mean bootstrap, rng(20261114 + n) for the ratio bootstrap. Fig. 6c and point jitter: one default_rng(6) shared across panels a-c.
- TEXT-GAP: implementation is not named: sign-flip tests, bootstrap and Benjamini-Hochberg are custom NumPy code (moved to `panels.py`), not a library routine; recomputed with CPython 3.12.4, numpy 2.2.6, pandas 2.3.3 (originally CPython 3.10.9, numpy 1.26.4).
- TEXT-GAP: all tests are two-sided; the sign-flip statistic is the mean paired difference with |null mean| >= |observed mean| - 1e-12 counted as extreme; no degrees of freedom apply.
- TEXT-GAP: tests with fewer than eight paired participants remain in their family with p = 1 (stated in the A24/A28 reports, not in v28d).
- TEXT-GAP: per-stratum n for d-f: CapScan n = 15 in every displayed region (fluid and stool), PREDICT n = 93.

- TEXT-GAP: the manuscript names the seed (20261004) and draw count but not the generator (NumPy default_rng, PCG64), the interval type (percentile, 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles), or the stratum order within the first stream (PREDICT, CapScan all, CapScan stool, intestinal fluid); the exact stool upper bound -2.26 depends on that order (Codex N0206 drew -2.47 with another order) (STATS.tsv rows 1-2).
- TEXT-GAP: four PREDICT acquisitions without a patient identifier are treated as their own bootstrap clusters (97 clusters = 93 patients + 4); the manuscript does not state this (STATS.tsv row 1).
- TEXT-GAP: the software of the bootstrap (Python 3.12.4, NumPy 2.2.6, pandas 2.3.3) is not stated; Methods line 777 names Python 3.10.9 and NumPy 1.26.4 only for the pooled rescoring (STATS.tsv all rows).
- TEXT-GAP: the Abstract "21–36% more in intestinal fluid" is the range of the four regional medians (colon 21.1%, jejunum 35.8%); a reader can link it only through Results line 123. The pooled intestinal-fluid median is 28.3% (Results line 133). Suggested v28f wording if the authors want it explicit: "21–36% more across four intestinal sampling regions" (AUTHOR decision; no value changes) (NUMBERS.tsv rows 6-7).
- TEXT-GAP: "median database-selection times of 1–3 min per acquisition" (line 193) is computed here as per-acquisition sum of the lookup-extraction (05_persample) and parsimony (06_parsimony) task times, median per cohort (1.23-3.21 min); the sum of the two stage medians gives 1.12-3.14 min. Both round to 1–3; the definition is not stated (NUMBERS.tsv rows 16-17).
- AUTHOR: none of the Abstract or Discussion numbers here carries a reference to Source Data; the Abstract gains rely on Results lines 123 and 133 and Methods line 777.

### methods

- TEXT-GAP: line 267 does not state how the spectrum-split intervals were built; from the code: percentile 95% interval, 10,000 draws shared by all comparisons of a stratum, whole patients or participants resampled within the stratum, seed 20261003 (STATS.tsv rows 267).
- TEXT-GAP: line 275 does not state the seeds or the test statistic; from the code: percentile patient bootstrap with seed 20260907, Monte Carlo sign flips in 100 batches of 2,000 with seeds 20260907 (human-assigned share) and 20260908 (protein-group depth), statistic = mean on-minus-off difference, P = (exceedances + 1) / 200,001 with 67 and 250 exceedances (STATS.tsv rows 275).
- TEXT-GAP: line 267 does not state the seed of the cross-fit participant bootstrap (20261004 in analyze_final.py); the intervals belong to figures/Fig4 and are not recomputed here.
- AUTHOR: line 299 "64-thread" is the lake-builder thread setting; the node has 128 logical CPUs. The text is correct as written but could say "64 threads on a 128-CPU, 512-GB node".
- AUTHOR: line 233 says the five PREDICT files were reconverted "before all searches reported here"; the second-engine searches (final/second_engine_all) used the truncated files, so this holds only if no second-engine PREDICT result is reported.
- AUTHOR: line 205 reads acquisition settings "from the instrument methods embedded in all 331 RAW files"; the v28f CapScan roster has 347 acquisitions and the 16 participant-15 RAW files were not checked (ANSWERS_A item 9). Not recomputed in this unit.

### supplementary_methods_1_5

- TEXT-GAP: line 553, permuted-prediction control: one permutation per acquisition (Python random.Random, seed 20261005 plus the character-code sum of the acquisition name); pooled 45 exact matches of 801,613 reference identifications (0.0056%); per acquisition 0 to 0.017%.
- TEXT-GAP: line 657, CapScan own-stool test (Codex S107): two-sided permutation test of the mean own-stool advantage (mean Hellinger distance of a participant's four intestinal-region OG profiles to other participants' stool minus to own stool), 99,999 random permutations of stool labels across participants, seed 20260910, P = (1 + count) / 100,000; n = 15 participants (v28f). P = 1 × 10⁻⁵ is the smallest value this test can return. Twelve tests (4 rules x 3 prevalence filters 0, 0.1, 0.2), no multiplicity correction. Acquisitions are averaged within specimen and specimens within participant-region before the test.
- TEXT-GAP: line 657, PREDICT cross-omics sensitivity (Codex S108): Mantel test with Spearman correlation of Euclidean distances between Hellinger-transformed profiles (metabolites log10, features present in at least 70% of patients, median-imputed, z-scored), two-sided, 1,999 permutations of patients, seed 20260907, n = 77 patients; no multiplicity correction in the sensitivity (the production analysis applies Benjamini-Hochberg within families of 28). The 0.16-0.64 range covers the three restricted rules and the DNA, RNA and metabolite layers over KO, OG and pathway levels (27 tests); all 60 rows are in STATS.tsv.
- TEXT-GAP: line 657, healthy-stool KO correlations (Codex S109): median over acquisition pairs (66 technical-replicate pairs, 45 inter-individual pairs) of the Pearson r of log2 KO intensity over KOs positive in both acquisitions; descriptive. The printed ranges 0.89-0.91 and 0.69-0.73 pool the profile with and without human-assigned signal; with one profile alone the ranges are 0.90-0.91 and 0.70-0.73 (all) or 0.89-0.90 and 0.69-0.73 (human-assigned withheld).
- TEXT-GAP: line 657, "allocated a median of 7-61% instead of 62-81%": medians over acquisitions of allocated / total LFQ intensity in the profile with human-assigned signal withheld, per cohort (58 healthy stool, 306 PREDICT, 346 non-empty CapScan), level and rule.
- TEXT-GAP: line 657, "88-95% of compatible proteins": share of compatible proteins whose MGnify annotation was joined by gene identifier with a verified sequence, before the seed-ortholog check, over the five CAMPI arms and the three cohorts (88.3% PREDICT to 94.7% CAMPI reference; CapScan 89.3% on 331 and 89.2% on 347 acquisitions).
- AUTHOR: line 657 says the sensitivity values were calculated before the seed-ortholog check. The HSTOOL and PREDICT values come from the 2026-10-03 run on the 2026-10-01 annotated-origin consensus; the CapScan 347 rerun used the participant-15 consensus built with the same 2026-10-01 rules. That this consensus also predates the check was not verified here.
- AUTHOR: the Source Data package does not contain the AlphaNovo accuracy table, the partial-origin rows, the input-completeness table, the member-presence table or the allocation-sensitivity tables, and its Methods_records/M07_parser_audit sheets hold the 710-file version. The tables in source_data/supplementary_methods_1_5/ (and Fig2, SFig02) can fill these sheets.

### supplementary_methods_6_7

- AUTHOR: Line 751 "452,255 for CapScan" is the union of the 331 v28d FASTAs; with the v28f 347-acquisition roster it is 460,977 (union_contributions.tsv). Status v28f_change:460,977 follows the decision recorded in text_statistics/methods (line 269); confirm.
- AUTHOR: Lines 755-761 (spectrum split) are kept on the v28d 710-acquisition roster (CapScan 331), as are the v28f Supplementary Fig. 6a-c. The 726-acquisition re-run gives CapScan 347 acquisitions, 15 participants (all 347), and two zero-yield CapScan disjoint arms instead of one; if v28f adopts it, "331" (lines 755, 757) and "One low-yield CapScan disjoint arm" (line 761) change. The heuristic-fallback count of the re-run is not in its collector outputs (UNTRACED for the 347 alternative).
- AUTHOR: The cross-fit numbers of lines 775-777 come from the iteration-4 run used by the Source Data package and the v28f Fig. 4b,c build. The Codex recheck used the CapScan re-run pooled over 347 acquisitions and gets 25 and 27 (not 28) perturbation-moved acquisitions and a stool 9-50 upper bound of −2.47% (not −2.3%); state which run is the reference.
- TEXT-GAP: Line 733 does not state the bootstrap seed (2026091051), the interval type (percentile, 2.5% and 97.5% quantiles) or the generator (numpy default_rng, one stream through the eight summaries in fixed order) (STATS.tsv simulation rows).
- TEXT-GAP: Lines 713-715 do not state the two fusion-control seeds (20260907, 20260908), and the ranges are between-seed ranges, not intervals (STATS.tsv fusion rows).
- TEXT-GAP: Lines 727-729 do not state the three FDRBench entrapment seeds (20260910, 20260911, 20260912) (STATS.tsv donor rows).
- TEXT-GAP: Line 765 does not state the seed of the single label permutation (0, numpy default_rng, one stream through acquisitions in file-name order) (STATS.tsv permutation row).
- TEXT-GAP: Line 777 does not state that the 95% intervals are percentile intervals, that acquisitions without a participant identifier are their own cluster, or the environment of the bootstrap summaries (Python 3.12.4, NumPy 2.2.6, pandas 2.3.3; the line states Python 3.10.9 and NumPy 1.26.4, which applies to the pooled rescoring) (STATS.tsv cross-fit rows).
- TEXT-GAP: Line 723 (additional-confidence fusion summaries) states no seed values; the seeds are those of lines 713-715. No number is printed there, so nothing is recomputed (register row S011).

### supplementary_methods_8_11

- TEXT-GAP: CapScan regional and own-stool bootstrap intervals use seed 20261003 (2,000 participant resamples); the own-stool interval is not described at all in Supplementary Methods 8 (STATS.tsv own-stool rows).
- TEXT-GAP: functional-profile permutations use the same seed as native profiles, 20260907; line 815 states it for native profiles only, lines 827 and 835 do not.
- TEXT-GAP: held-out EDS prediction: label permutations are drawn with seed 20260915 (the first split seed) and the patient bootstrap uses seed 20260918; neither is stated (line 803-805).
- TEXT-GAP: antibiotic sign flips use seed 20260907 plus the endpoint index (tables/STab3/panels.py), not stated at line 795.
- TEXT-GAP: histidine and terminal-fraction tests: sign-flip and bootstrap seeds (A28: 20260914 + n and 20261014 + n; A24: sha256-derived per n) and bootstrap draw counts per endpoint are not stated; exact enumeration for n ≤ 16 is stated.
- TEXT-GAP: individual-function correlations use scipy.stats.spearmanr (two-sided, t approximation, df = n − 2); implementation not stated (line 829).
- TEXT-GAP: no interval is computed for the cross-omics, controlled-distance or individual-function correlations; the text neither states intervals nor their absence.
- TEXT-GAP: the evenness and leading-share tests (Results line 159) use scipy.stats.wilcoxon with method="exact"; statistic (W = 5 and W = 3), implementation and correction family (none) are not stated.
- TEXT-GAP: statistical software versions for the downstream analyses are not stated (Python 3.12.4, numpy 2.2.6, scipy 1.16.3, statsmodels 0.14.5, scikit-learn 1.7.2 for the biology analyses; Python 3.10.9, numpy 1.26.4, scipy 1.12.0 for the histidine and terminal-fraction inference). Line 907 lists search and annotation software only.
